1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - Node E
   - Node H

2. **Identify all paths between E and H:**
   - E -> B -> H
   - E -> F -> G -> H
   - E -> F -> X -> G -> H
   - E -> F -> X -> K -> G -> H

3. **Determine the minimal set of edges to remove:**
   - Removing the edge (E, F) will break paths that pass through F.
   - Removing the edge (G, H) will break paths that pass through G.
   - Removing the edge (B, H) will break the direct path E -> B -> H.
   - Removing the edges (E, B) and (F, G) in combination would also work.
   - Removing both E -> F and G -> H achieves the goal with the least amount of edges.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, F), (G, H)].

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph such that the two black nodes (N and G) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these nodes and find the nodes whose removal will disrupt all possible paths between them.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are labeled N and G.

2. **Identify Paths Between N and G:**
   - One possible path is: N -> B -> H -> G
   - Another possible path is: N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G
   - Another possible path is: N -> B -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> D -> P -> O -> C -> I -> P -> J -> M -> E -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G
   - Another possible path is: N -> Y -> O -> C -> I -> P -> J -> D -> P -> O -> C -> I -> P -> J -> M -> E -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G
   - Another possible path is: N -> Y -> O -> C -> I -> P -> J -> M -> E -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G

3. **Determine the Minimum Cut:**
   - Removing node B will disconnect N from the path N -> B -> H -> G and N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G.
   - Removing node H will disconnect G from the path N -> B -> H -> G and N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G.
   - Removing node F will disconnect the path N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G.
   - Removing node X or K will disconnect the path N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G.

By analyzing these paths, we see that removing node N and node G is not an option as per the problem statement. To disconnect all paths between N and G, we need to consider removing nodes that are common in all possible paths.

### Cut Set Analysis:
- By removing nodes B and H, we disrupt the most direct paths.
- By removing node F, we disrupt another major path.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to be removed is 2, with the cut set being [B, H].

### Final Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, H]
